. This is precisely the complex
8.9
we find in the last section.

In summary, by performing KK reduction directly on
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, we will end up with the same answer as in the last section using open-closed coupling. This is expected given the self consistency of the open-closed string theory.

8.4
Holomorphic Chern-Simons in deformed geometry, II

In the last section, we discussed how the quadratic terms of the KK theory are modified in the deformed geometry. These terms correspond to a deformed differential of the complex of KK fields. In fact, all the products and higher products constructed in Section
4.4
that build the action functional of KK theory are deformed in the new geometry. For example, we commented on the higher order open-closed coupling of the KK theory in Section
7.5
. By letting
\bm{\beta}[0,0]=N\epsilon\partial_{z}
in the formula
7.44
, we find a deformed action of the form
\int BA\partial_{z}A
.

To correctly obtain all deformation, we need to use all the data in the special deformation retract (SDR). Recall from Section
4.1
that the SDR we used to build the product and higher product on CR cohomology is given by the following

h\hbox to29.98pt{\vbox to32.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.7693pt\lower-14.45697pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

(8.22)

Using homological perturbation theory, we obtain the new SDR after adding
N\epsilon\partial_{z}
to the differential

h^{\prime}\hbox to29.98pt{\vbox to32.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.7693pt\lower-14.45697pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\Omega^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}_{3d}\otimes\Omega_{b}^{0,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(S^{3}),\hat{d}+\bar{\partial}_{b}+N\epsilon\partial_{z})\overset{p^{\prime}}{\underset{i^{\prime}}{\rightleftarrows}}(\Omega_{3d}\otimes H_{b}^{0,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(S^{3}),\hat{d}+D).

(8.23)

The differential
D
is given in the previous section. The other operations
h^{\prime},p^{\prime},i^{\prime}
are given by

\displaystyle h^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 0}(N^{k}\tilde{h}^{k}\partial_{z}^{k})h,

(8.24)

\displaystyle p^{\prime}

\displaystyle=p(\sum_{k\geq 0}N^{k}\tilde{h}^{k}\partial_{z}^{k}),

\displaystyle i^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 0}(N^{k}\tilde{h}^{k}\partial_{z}^{k})i.

The new
A_{\infty}
structure on
\Omega_{3d}\otimes H_{b}^{0,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(S^{3})
is given by the homotopy transfer theorem. They can be computed by the same method as in Section
4.3
, but now with the new maps
i^{\prime},p^{\prime},h^{\prime}
.

For instance, the deformed product
m_{2}^{\prime}
is defined by
m_{2}^{\prime}(-,-)=p^{\prime}\circ M(i^{\prime}(-),i^{\prime}(-))
. For the purpose of obtaining the corresponding deformed action, it suffices to compute it on
(\Omega^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}_{3d}\otimes H^{0,0}_{CR}(S^{3}))^{\otimes 2}
. In this case,
m_{2}^{\prime}
is simplified to

m_{2}^{\prime}(f_{1}Y_{1},f_{2}Y_{2})=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\geq 0}N^{k_{1}+k_{2}}(\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}f_{1})(\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}f_{2})pM(\tilde{h}^{k_{1}}Y_{1},\tilde{h}^{k_{2}}Y_{2}),\quad f_{i}\in\Omega^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}_{3d},Y_{i}\in H^{0,0}_{CR}(S^{3}).

(8.25)

We denote the map
m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2})}(Y_{1},Y_{2})=pM(\tilde{h}^{k_{1}}Y_{1},\tilde{h}^{k_{2}}Y_{2})
, then

m_{2}^{\prime}(f_{1}Y_{1},f_{2}Y_{2})=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\geq 0}N^{k_{1}+k_{2}}(\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}f_{1})(\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}f_{2})m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2})}(Y_{1},Y_{2}).

(8.26)

We have that
m_{2}^{(0,0)}=m_{2}
by definition. We illustrate the computation of
m_{2}^{(1,0)}
and
m_{2}^{(0,1)}
. To compute
m_{2}^{(1,0)}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})
, we first compute
\tilde{h}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})
. Using the definition
7.19
of
\tilde{h}
and expand the harmonic polynomial into polynomials of
w_{i},\bar{w}_{i}
, we find

\tilde{h}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})=\frac{1}{p+q}(pw_{1}^{p-1}w_{2}^{q}\bar{w}_{2}-qw_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q-1}\bar{w}_{1}).

(8.27)

Therefore,

m_{2}^{(1,0)}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})=\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q)(p+q+r+s)}w_{1}^{p+r-1}w_{2}^{q+s-1}.

(8.28)

Similarly,
m_{2}^{(0,1)}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})=-\frac{ps-qr}{(r+s)(p+q+r+s)}w_{1}^{p+r-1}w_{2}^{q+s-1}
. Using these deformed products, we obtain the first-order deformed action functional

\displaystyle I^{(2,1)}_{O}=

\displaystyle N\sum_{p,q,r,s}\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q+r+s)}\left(\frac{1}{p+q}\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r-1,q+s-1][\partial_{z}\mathbf{A}[p,q],\mathbf{A}[r,s]]\right.

(8.29)

\displaystyle-\left.\frac{1}{r+s}\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r-1,q+s-1][\mathbf{A}[p,q],\partial_{z}\mathbf{A}[r,s]]\right).

In order to compute all other
m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2})}
, one can use our formula
4.29
for the product of two arbitrary
S^{3}
harmonics. Here, we provide a different method. Note that
m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2})}
is compatible with the
SU(2)
action and send
\mathcal{H}_{j_{1},0}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{j_{2},0}
to
\mathcal{H}_{j_{1}+j_{2}-k_{1}-k_{2},0}
. Therefore the corresponding matrix elements must be proportional to the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients in the orthonormal basis. To determine the constant of proportionality it suffices to compute one non-zero value of
m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2})}
. First, using
8.16
we find that
\tilde{h}^{k}(e^{(j)}_{m})=\sqrt{\frac{(2j-k)!}{(2j)!k!}}e^{(j-\frac{k}{2},\frac{k}{2})}_{m}
. The easiest case of a non-zero product is the product between the highest weight vector
e^{(j-\frac{k}{2},\frac{k}{2})}_{j}
and the lowest weight vector. We have

\displaystyle m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2})}(e^{(j_{1})}_{j_{1}},e^{(j_{2})}_{-j_{2}})=\sqrt{\frac{(2j_{1}-k_{1})!(2j_{2}-k_{2})!}{k_{1}!k_{2}!(2j_{1})!(2j_{2})!}}m_{2}(e^{(j_{1}-\frac{k_{1}}{2},\frac{k_{1}}{2})}_{j_{1}},e^{(j_{2}-\frac{k_{2}}{2},\frac{k_{2}}{2})}_{-j_{2}})

(8.30)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k_{2}}\frac{\sqrt{(2j_{1}+1)(2j_{2}+1)}(2j_{1}-k_{1})!(2j_{2}-k_{2})!}{k_{1}!k_{2}!(2j_{1}+2j_{2}-k_{1}-k_{2}+1)!}\sqrt{\frac{(2j_{1}+2j_{2}-2k_{1}-2k_{2}+1)!}{(2j_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})!(2j_{2}-k_{1}-k_{2})!}}e^{(j_{1}+j_{2}-k_{1}-k_{2})}_{j_{1}-j_{2}}.

This implies

\displaystyle m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2})}(e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2})}_{m_{2}})=

\displaystyle(-1)^{k_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{(2j_{1}+1)(2j_{2}+1)(2j_{1}+2j_{2}-2k_{1}-2k_{2})!}{(2j_{1}+2j_{2}-k_{1}-k_{2}+1)!(2j_{1})!(2j_{2})!(2j_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})!(2j_{2}-k_{1}-k_{2})!}}

(8.31)

\displaystyle\times\frac{(2j_{1}-k_{1})!(2j_{2}-k_{2})!}{k_{1}!k_{2}!}C^{j_{1},j_{2},j_{1}+j_{2}-k_{1}-k_{2}}_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{1}+m_{2}}e^{(j_{1}+j_{2}-k_{1}-k_{2})}_{m_{1}+m_{2}}.

Transforming this result into the unnormalized basis
\{w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q}\}
(see formula (2.18) in
[
42
]
), we have

m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2})}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})=\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}}R_{k}(p,q,r,s)w_{1}^{p+r-k}w_{2}^{q+s-k}}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k+1]_{k}},\quad k=k_{1}+k_{2},

(8.32)

where we used the descending Pochhammer symbol
[a]_{n}=\frac{a!}{(a-n)!}
. The constant
R_{k}(p,q,r,s)

7
7

7

This constant also appears in the definition of
W_{\infty}
algebra
[
42
]
, and we are following the notation of
[
25
]

is defined by

R_{k}(p,q,r,s):=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}\binom{k}{i}[p]_{k-i}[q]_{i}[r]_{i}[s]_{k-i}.

(8.33)

Using this deformed product, we can write down the deformed cubic action of all orders:

\displaystyle I^{(2,k)}_{O}=

\displaystyle N^{k}\sum_{p,q,r,s}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}}R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k+1]_{k}}

(8.34)

\displaystyle\times\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r-k,q+s-k][\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\mathbf{A}[p,q],\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{A}[r,s]].

We can check that by taking
k=1
in the above formula, we reproduce the first order deformed action
I^{(2,1)}_{O}
in
8.29
.

As we have mentioned, the whole
A_{\infty}
algebra structure on
\Omega_{3d}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}\otimes H_{b}^{0,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(S^{3})
is deformed in the new geometry. They can be computed by the same method presented in this paper. Understanding the deformed
A_{\infty}
structure allows us to have a complete expansion of the action functional in the deformed
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
geometry. This can, in principle, give us the full tree level boundary chiral algebra.

8.5
Kodaira-Spencer gravity in deformed geometry

Analogous to the holomorphic Chern-Simons theory, we can also understand Kodaira-Spencer gravity in deformed geometry as a deformation of the theory on flat space. There are two differences in the analysis. Firstly, we add the following differential to the complex:

\{N\epsilon\partial_{z},-\}.

(8.35)

Secondly, we use the homotopy operator
\mathds{h}
defined in
7.38
in the homological perturbation. After passing to the CR cohomology, the deformed differential is given by

\displaystyle\mathds{D}

\displaystyle=(1-\{N\epsilon\partial_{z},-\}\circ\mathds{h})^{-1}\circ\{N\epsilon\partial_{z},-\}

(8.36)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathds{D}^{(k)},

where

\mathds{D}^{(k)}=(\{N\epsilon\partial_{z},-\}\circ\mathds{h})^{k-1}\circ\{N\epsilon\partial_{z},-\}.

(8.37)

This deformed differential corresponds to the following deformed quadratic action

\int\partial^{-1}\bm{\alpha}\mathds{D}\bm{\alpha}.

(8.38)

To compute this deformed action, we need to compute the action of
\mathds{D}
on the fields. We have

\displaystyle\mathds{D}^{(k)}(f(z)Y(w_{i},\bar{w}_{i})\partial_{z})

\displaystyle=N^{k}\partial_{z}^{k}(f)\tilde{h}^{k-1}(Y)\epsilon\partial_{z},

(8.39)

\displaystyle\mathds{D}^{(k)}(f(z)Y(w_{i},\bar{w}_{i})\hat{\eta})

\displaystyle=N^{k}\partial_{z}^{k}(f)\tilde{h}^{k-1}(Y)\epsilon\hat{\eta}-2kN^{k}\partial_{z}^{k-1}(f)\tilde{h}^{k-1}(Y)\epsilon\partial_{z},

\displaystyle\mathds{D}^{(k)}(f(z)Y(w_{i},\bar{w}_{i})\eta)

\displaystyle=N^{k}\partial_{z}^{k}(f)\tilde{h}^{k-1}(Y)\epsilon\eta-(k-1)N^{k}\partial^{k}_{z}(f)\tilde{h}^{k}(Y)\epsilon\hat{\eta}

\displaystyle+k(k-1)N^{k}\partial_{z}^{k-1}(f)\tilde{h}^{k}(Y)\partial_{z}.

Thus we can compute

\displaystyle\mathds{D}^{(k)}(\bm{\alpha})=\sum_{p,q}N^{k}(p+q)

\displaystyle\left(k\partial^{k}_{z}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\tilde{h}^{k}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})\epsilon\hat{\eta}-\partial^{k}_{z}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\tilde{h}^{k-1}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})\epsilon\eta\right.

(8.40)

\displaystyle\left.-k(k+1)\partial^{k-1}_{z}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\tilde{h}^{k}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})\epsilon\partial_{z}\right).

Using the above formula, we can expand the deformed action
\int\Tr\partial^{-1}\bm{\alpha}\mathds{D}\bm{\alpha}
. The constant coefficient in front of the term
\int\bm{\alpha}[q,p]dz\partial_{z}^{k-1}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]
is computed by
(p+q)k(k+1)\Tr_{S^{3}}(w_{1}^{q}w_{2}^{p}\tilde{h}^{k}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q}))
. In fact, this is already computed in
8.17
,
8.18
. Therefore, we have the following deformed quadratic term

I^{(1,k)}_{C}=\sum_{p+q=k}N^{k}(-1)^{q}\frac{p!q!}{((k-1)!)^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\int\bm{\alpha}[q,p]dz\partial_{z}^{k-1}\bm{\alpha}[p,q].

(8.41)

Using the same method as we analyze the deformed cubic action for holomorphic Chern-Simons, we obtain the following deformed cubic action for Kodaira-Spencer gravity

I^{(2,k)}_{C}=\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\int\Tr\bm{\beta}(\mathds{h}\circ\mathds{D}^{(k_{1})}(\bm{\alpha}))(\mathds{h}\circ\mathds{D}^{(k_{2})}(\bm{\alpha})),

(8.42)

where we assumed that
\mathds{h}\circ\mathds{D}^{(k)}=\mathrm{Id}
for
k=0
. Using the expansion
8.40
of
\mathds{D}^{(k)}(\bm{\alpha})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathds{h}\circ\mathds{D}^{(k)}(\bm{\alpha})=\sum_{p,q}N^{k}(p+q)

\displaystyle\left((k+1)\partial^{k}_{z}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\tilde{h}^{k+1}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})\hat{\eta}-\partial^{k}_{z}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\tilde{h}^{k}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})\eta\right.

(8.43)

\displaystyle\left.-k(k+1)\partial^{k-1}_{z}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\tilde{h}^{k+1}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})\partial_{z}\right).

We can expand the deformed cubic action using the above formula. The constant coefficient in front of the term
\int\bm{\beta}[p+r-k-1,q+s-k-1]\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\bm{\alpha}[r,s]
can be computed using the deformed product
m_{2}^{(k_{1}+1,k_{2})}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})
and
m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2}+1)}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})
obtained in the last section. We have the following

\displaystyle I^{(2,k)}_{C}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}}(p+q)(r+s)R_{k+1}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q+r+s-k]_{k+1}}\left(\frac{1}{[p+q]_{k_{1}+1}[r+s]_{k_{2}}}+\frac{1}{[p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}+1}}\right)

(8.44)

\displaystyle\times\int\bm{\beta}[p+r-k-1,q+s-k-1]\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\bm{\alpha}[r,s].

8.6
Open-closed coupling in deformed geometry

The open-closed coupling terms studied in Section
7.5
are also deformed in the
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
background. To understand the deformation, we first understand the flat space coupling as coming from a bracket of the complex
\ker\partial\cap\mathrm{PV}^{1,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(X)\oplus\Omega^{0,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(X)

\displaystyle\{-,-\}:\mathrm{PV}^{1,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(X)\otimes\Omega^{0,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(X)

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{0,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(X),

(8.45)

\displaystyle\bm{\mu}^{i}\partial_{z_{i}}\otimes\bm{\gamma}

\displaystyle\mapsto\bm{\mu}^{i}\partial_{z_{i}}(\bm{\gamma}).

Remark 8.1
.

Only the leading order coupling of the
\mathrm{PV}^{1,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}(X)
part of Kodaira-Spencer fields and the holomorphic Chern-Simons can be understood as a
2
-bracket. Other parts of the coupling correspond to
L_{\infty}
maps on the space of fields
[
17
]
.

Given this bracket that encodes (part of) the open-closed coupling, we can use the same techniques as before to compute the deformed action. For example, we have the following deformed coupling terms with fields
\mathbf{B},\mathbf{A},\bm{\alpha}

I_{OC}^{(2,k)}[\mathbf{B},\mathbf{A},\bm{\alpha}]=\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\int\Tr\mathbf{B}\left(\mathds{h}\circ\mathds{D}^{k_{1}}(\bm{\alpha})(N^{k_{2}}\tilde{h}^{k_{2}}\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{A})\right).

(8.46)

Using the expansion
8.43
for
\mathds{h}\circ\mathds{D}^{(k_{1})}(\bm{\alpha})
and the deformed product
m_{2}^{(k_{1}+1,k_{2})}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})
and
m_{2}^{(k_{1},k_{2}+1)}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})
obtained in the previous section, we can easily expand the above deformed action as follows

\displaystyle I_{OC}^{(2,k)}[\mathbf{B},\mathbf{A},\bm{\alpha}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\left(\frac{r+s-2k_{2}}{p+q-k_{1}}+1\right)\frac{(p+q)(-1)^{k_{2}}R_{k+1}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k]_{k+1}}

(8.47)

\displaystyle\times\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r-k-1,q+s-k-1]\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{A}[r,s]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+k_{2}=k\\
k_{1}\geq 1,k_{2}\leq k-1\end{subarray}}\frac{(p+q)(-1)^{k_{2}}R_{k+1}(p,q,r,s)}{(k_{1}-1)!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}+1}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k]_{k+1}}

\displaystyle\times\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r-k-1,q+s-k-1]\partial_{z}^{k_{1}-1}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\partial_{z}^{k_{2}+1}\mathbf{A}[r,s].

By a change of summation variable:
k_{1}\rightarrow k_{1}+1,k_{2}\rightarrow k_{2}-1
, the second term in the above formula can be rewritten as

\frac{-k_{2}(r+s-k_{2}+1)}{(p+q-k_{1})(p+q-k_{1}-1)}\frac{(p+q)(-1)^{k_{2}}R_{k+1}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k]_{k+1}}\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[...]\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{A}[r,s].

(8.48)

Since the coefficient in the above formula vanishes for
k_{2}=0
, the summation range can be taken to be all
k_{1}+k_{2}=k
. Thus we can rewrite the deformed coupling as follows

\displaystyle I_{OC}^{(2,k)}[\mathbf{B},\mathbf{A},\bm{\alpha}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\frac{(p+q-k-1)}{(p+q-1)}\frac{(p+q)(-1)^{k_{2}}R_{k+1}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q-2]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k-1]_{k}}

(8.49)

\displaystyle\times\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r-k-1,q+s-k-1]\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\bm{\alpha}[p,q]\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{A}[r,s].

Similarly, we can write down the deformed coupling between the fields
\bm{\beta},\mathbf{A}

\displaystyle I_{OC}^{(2,k)}[\mathbf{A},\mathbf{A},\bm{\beta}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}}R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{!}!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k+1]_{k}}

(8.50)

\displaystyle\times\left(\int\bm{\beta}[p+r-k,q+s-k]\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\mathbf{A}[p,q]\partial_{z}^{k_{2}+1}\mathbf{A}[r,s]\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{r+s-2k_{2}}{p+q+r+s-2k}\int\partial_{z}\bm{\beta}[p+r-k,q+s-k]\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\mathbf{A}[p,q]\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{A}[r,s]\right).

There are also coupling between the fields
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}
and the other parts of the Kodaira-Spencer fields. We leave them to future analysis.

8.7
Moyal-Weyl product and higher spin

In our previous analysis, we observe that the constant
R_{k}(p,q,r,s)

8.33
plays an important role in the construction of the cubic action for various KK theories in the deformed geometry. In this section, we briefly recall its connection with higher spin theory.

First, we recall the definition of the Moyal-Weyl product, which is a non-commutative deformation of the commutative product of functions on
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
given by a constant Poisson tensor. In our case, we focus on the Moyal-Weyl deformation of
\mathbb{C}[w_{1},w_{2}]
equipped with the Poisson tensor
\epsilon_{ij}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}
. The Moyal-Weyl product
\star
is given by

f\star g:=m(e^{\frac{\hbar}{2}\epsilon_{ij}\partial_{i}\otimes\partial_{j}}f\otimes g),\quad f,g\in\mathbb{C}[w_{1},w_{2}],

(8.51)

where
m
is the commutative product map
m(a\otimes b)=ab
, and the exponential is treated as a power series. Expanding the exponential to the first order, one can find

(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q})\star(w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})=w_{1}^{p+r}w_{2}^{q+s}+\frac{\hbar}{2}(ps-qr)w_{1}^{p+r-1}w_{2}^{q+s-1}+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2}).

(8.52)

It is straightforward to expand the exponential to all orders, and we find that the Moyal-Weyl product is given by

(w_{1}^{l}w_{2}^{m})\star(w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s})=\sum_{k}(\frac{\hbar}{2})^{k}\frac{1}{k!}R_{k}(p,q,r,s)w_{1}^{p+r-k}w_{2}^{q+s-k}.

(8.53)

In fact, The Moyal-Weyl product and the constant
R_{k}(p,q,r,s)
appear in many higher spin related contexts. The global symmetry algebra of higher spin theory- the higher spin algebra
\mathfrak{hs}
can be defined using the Moyal-Weyl algebra. Vasiliev’s formulation of higher spin equation is constructed based on the Moyal-Weyl algebra, which controls its first-order interaction. The constant
R_{k}(p,q,r,s)
also appears in the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{\infty}
algebra, which is the chiral algebra of higher spin current
[
43
]
.

Indeed, our KK theory obtained by compactification is naturally a higher spin theory in three dimension. When we perform the compactification from
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, the KK theory can be understood as a higher spin theory on
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})/SU(2)
, which is precisely the Euclidean
AdS_{3}
. Therefore, it is not a coincidence that the same constant
R_{k}(p,q,r,s)
appears in the cubic interactions of the KK theory.

The KK theory we obtained contains an infinite series of effective interactions beyond cubic interactions. This is the same for the Vasiliev’s theory of higher spin. Though Vasiliev’s higher spin theory does not have an action functional, the
L_{\infty}
algebra controlling the equation of motion is nontrivial and contains an infinite tower of higher brackets. The
2
-bracket is, of course, defined by the Moyal bracket. The
3
-bracket is first constructed in
[
44
]
and is observed in
[
45
]
to correspond to the Feigin–Felder–Shoikhet cocycle
[
46
]
of Weyl algebra. This
3
-bracket can also be represented as a specific correlator in a topological quantum mechanics of a free particle on the circle
[
47
]
. It will be an interesting problem to see if the quadratic vertex of our KK theory given by a
3
-bracket on the fields can have a similar interpretation.

9
Twisted holography from boundary chiral algebra

In this section, we revisit a model of twisted holography proposed in
[
7
]
. This model captures a protected subsector of the canonical example of
AdS_{5}/CFT_{4}
duality
[
1
,
2
]
. The “gravity” side of the duality is the string field theory of
B
-model topological string on a
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
background. The “gauge theory” side of the duality involves a large
N
chiral algebra of gauged
\beta\gamma
systems. In this paper, we present nontrivial results in matching the chiral algebras from both sides of the duality.

9.1
Chiral algebra on topological D-branes

In this section, we introduce the large
N
chiral algebra studied in
[
7
]
. This is the world volume theory on a stack of
N
topological B-branes wrapping
\mathbb{C}\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}
with the inclusion of
K|K
space-filling branes. This chiral algebra can also be regarded as coming from the
4d

\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal field theory through the procedure of
[
48
]
. In one word, this is the ”twisted” version of the gauge theory side of the AdS/CFT correspondence.

The chiral algebra involves of the following system

•

A
bc
system
(b(z),c(z))
valued in
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
:

b(z)c(w)\sim\frac{1}{z-w}.

(9.1)

•

a
\beta\gamma
system (or sometime called symplectic boson)
(Z^{1}(z),Z^{2}(z))
valued in the adjoint
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
:

Z^{1}(z)Z^{2}(w)\sim\frac{1}{z-w}.

(9.2)

•

a
\beta\gamma
system
(I(z),J(z))
valued in the fundamental representation
\mathbb{C}^{K|K}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{N}
:

I(z)J(w)\sim\frac{1}{z-w}

(9.3)

.

The BRST operator takes the form

Q_{BRST}=\oint\Tr cZ^{1}Z^{2}+\Tr IcJ+\frac{1}{2}\Tr bc^{2}.

(9.4)

In the large
N
limit, the BRST reduction is exactly computed in
[
7
]
. The BRST cohomology comprises polynomials of the following single-trace operators.

\displaystyle A^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\Tr(Z^{(i_{1}}Z^{i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n})}),

(9.5)

\displaystyle B^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\Tr(bZ^{(i_{1}}Z^{i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n})}),

\displaystyle C^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\Tr(\partial cZ^{(i_{1}}Z^{i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n})}),

\displaystyle D^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{ij}\Tr(\partial Z^{i}Z^{(j}Z^{i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n})})+\dots,

\displaystyle E^{(n)}_{\mathfrak{t}}

\displaystyle=\Tr(\mathfrak{t}IZ^{(i_{1}}Z^{i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n})}J).

In the twisted holography setup, the
A,B,C,D
towers of operators correspond to the boundary operators of the Kodaira-Spencer theory, and the
E
tower of operators correspond to the boundary operators of the holomorphic Chern-Simons. Following our form of the twisted holography conjecture, we will see that this large
N
chiral algebra can be matched with the boundary chiral algebra of the corresponding KK theory with a proper choice of boundary condition.

9.2
Boundary algebras from holomorphic Chern-Simons

The “gravity” side of the duality is given by the Kodaira-Spencer gravity coupled with holomorphic Chern-Simons theory in the deformed geometry
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
. First, we focus on the holomorphic Chern-Simons part.

Recall that our
3d
fields have mode expansion

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}(t,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q\geq 0}\mathbf{A}[p,q](t,z,\bar{z})w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},

(9.6)

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}(t,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q\geq 0}\frac{(p+q+1)!}{p!q!}\mathbf{B}[p,q](t,z,\bar{z})\bar{w}_{1}^{p}\bar{w}_{2}^{q}\epsilon.

They are related to the original
6d
fields as follows

\boldsymbol{\EuScript{A}}=\sum_{p,q\geq 0}\mathbf{A}[p,q](r,z,\bar{z})z_{1}^{p}z_{2}^{q}+\frac{(p+q+1)!}{p!q!}\mathbf{B}[p,q](r,z,\bar{z})\frac{\bar{z}_{1}^{p}\bar{z}_{2}^{q}}{r^{2(p+q)}}\frac{\epsilon_{ij}\bar{z}_{i}d\bar{z}_{j}}{r^{4}}.

(9.7)

where
r=(z_{1}\bar{z}_{1}+z_{2}\bar{z}_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. We can see that the natural boundary condition we get by requiring the original
6d
fields to extend to
r=0
is to impose the following condition

\mathbf{B}[p,q]|_{t=0}=0\text{ for all }p,q.

(9.8)

This is the Neumann boundary condition for the
3d
KK theory. The corresponding boundary algebra should reproduce the chiral algebra of the original
6d
theory restricted along
\mathbb{C}
. We are more interested in the transverse boundary condition. This is the Dirichlet boundary condition given by

\mathbf{A}[p,q]|_{t=0}=0\text{ for all }p,q

(9.9)

According to the argument we presented in the introduction, the boundary algebra for this boundary condition should coincide with the universal defect algebra on
\mathbb{C}
. Then one of the prediction of twisted holography implies that this boundary chiral algebra is the same as the large
N
chiral algebra on B-brane, generated by the
E
towers of fields.

Remark 9.1
.

The above statement is actually not precise. Because the OPE of open string operators will eventually involve closed string operators, and the holomorphic Chern-Simons theory alone suffers from anomaly which requires the introduction of Kodaira-Spencer theory to cancel. A precise matching should involve the inclusion of Kodaira-Spencer gravity and their coupling. We provide some examples of matching the coupling terms and the corresponding B-brane OPE in
9.7
.

For the Dirichlet boundary condition, the (perturbative) boundary local operators are generated by the lowest component
B
of
\mathbf{B}
and its derivatives:

\partial_{z}^{n}B[p,q](z).

(9.10)

It is easy to see that there is a one-to-one correspondence of boundary local operators and the large
N
single trace operators from the
E
tower operators. Let

E^{(p,q)}=IZ^{(i_{1}}Z^{i_{2}}\dots Z^{i_{n})}J,\quad\text{where }\{i_{1},\dots i_{n}\}=\{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}^{p},\overbrace{2,\dots,2}^{q}\}.

(9.11)

Then we expect the following correspondence

\partial_{z}^{n}B[p,q](z)\Leftrightarrow\partial_{z}^{n}E^{(p,q)}(z).

(9.12)

In the next section, we provide some nontrivial results showing that OPE’s of the corresponding operators are also the same.

In fact, our
3d
KK theories are holomorphic topological cousins of the
3d
Chern-Simons theory. In this formulation, the twisted holography duality are reminiscent of the familiar holography duality between the Chern-Simons theory and the WZW model
[
49
]
.

9.3
Matching the OPE

In this section, we compare the OPE of the boundary chiral algebra and the large
N
chiral algebra. On the one side, the OPE for the chiral algebra on B-brane is computed by wick contraction of the matrix fields. On the other side, the boundary OPE is computed by Feynman diagrams through bulk interaction vertices. We will show that different wick contraction patterns have gravity interpretations as different bulk interaction vertices.

It is convenient to organize the
E
tower of fields into a generating function

E^{(n)}(\lambda;z):=I(z)Z(\lambda;z)^{n}J(z),

(9.13)

where

Z(\lambda;z):=Z^{1}(z)\lambda_{1}+Z^{2}(z)\lambda_{2}.

(9.14)

In this notation, the OPE between the
Z
fields can be written as

Z(\lambda;z)Z(\lambda^{\prime};0)\sim\frac{[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]}{z},

(9.15)

where
[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]=\lambda_{1}\lambda^{\prime}_{2}-\lambda_{2}\lambda^{\prime}_{1}
.
The generating field
E^{(n)}(x;z)
is related to the symmetrized operator
E^{(p,q)}(z)
as follows

E^{(n)}(\lambda;z)=\sum_{p+q=n}\binom{p+q}{p}E^{(p,q)}(z)\lambda_{1}^{p}\lambda_{2}^{q}.

(9.16)

9.3.1
Quadratic vertex and two-point function

In this section, we analyze the tree-level boundary OPE from the quadratic vertices of the KK theory. First, we briefly review how to perform such kind of computations in the
3d
holomorphic topological theories. We refer to
[
19
]
for a detailed analysis.

The kinetic term
\int\Tr\mathbf{B}\hat{d}\mathbf{A}
of the
3d
theory gives us a propagator connecting the superfields
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{A}
\mathbf{B}
\mathbf{A}

In the presence of boundary, the corresponding propagator is obtained using the method of reflection
[
19
]
. Omitting the color factor, we have

P_{\partial}(z,t;z^{\prime},t^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}(P(z,t;z^{\prime},t^{\prime})-P(z,t;z^{\prime},-t^{\prime})),

(9.17)

where
P(z,t;z^{\prime},t^{\prime})
is the pure bulk propagator

P(z,t;z^{\prime},t^{\prime})=\frac{1}{8\pi i}\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}^{\prime})d(t-t^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}(t-t^{\prime})d(\bar{z}-\bar{z}^{\prime})}{(|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}+(t-t^{\prime})^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}},

(9.18)

The action functional of the KK theory of holomorphic Chern-Simons on
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
consists of the flat space action functional and deformation terms coming from the deformed geometry. For the quadratic vertices, the flat space part
\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p,q]\hat{d}\mathbf{A}[p,q]
is the kinetic term which gives us the propagator connecting the fields
\mathbf{B}[p,q]
and
\mathbf{A}[p,q]
. The remaining part coming from
SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
deformation is analyzed in
8.2
. We have

I^{(1,k)}_{O}=\sum_{p+q=k-1}N^{k}\frac{(-1)^{p}}{(k-1)!}\frac{p!q!}{k!}\frac{1}{2}\int\Tr(\mathbf{A}[p,q]dz\partial_{z}^{k}\mathbf{A}[q,p]).

(9.19)

We have the following tree-level Feynman diagram associated with the vertex
I^{(1,k)}

I^{(1,p+q+1)_{O}}
\mathbf{B}[p,q]
\mathbf{B}[q,p]

The corresponding Feynman integral is computed by

\int_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C},s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}+s^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{z^{\prime}}^{p+q+1}\frac{1}{(|z^{\prime}|^{2}+s^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{z}sdsdz^{\prime}d\bar{z}^{\prime}.

(9.20)

The evaluation of this Feynman integral is given in Appendix
F
and we find

\frac{(p+q+1)!}{z^{p+q+2}}.

(9.21)

Therefore, the corresponding OPE is given by

B[p,q](z)B[q,p](0)\sim(-1)^{q}N^{p+q+1}\frac{p!q!}{(p+q)!}\frac{1}{z^{p+q+2}},

(9.22)

where we dropped the Lie algebra indices to simplify the notation. It is easy to restore them by adding the Killing form into the above formula.

On the B-brane side, this OPE should be matched with the full contraction of the
E
tower operators, which is also the two-point function

\langle E^{(n)}(\lambda,z)E^{(n)}(\lambda^{\prime},0)\rangle=\frac{N^{n+1}[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]^{n}}{z^{n+2}}.

(9.23)

Expanding
E^{(n)}(\lambda,z)
into the symmetrized operator
E^{(p,q)}
, we find

\langle E^{(p,q)}(z)E^{(q,p)}(0)\rangle=(-1)^{q}N^{p+q+1}\frac{p!q!}{(p+q)!}\frac{1}{z^{p+q+2}}.

(9.24)

This gives the same result as in the boundary chiral algebra. In fact, one should understand this matching of two-point functions as fixing the normalization of the operators on the two sides. The real non-trivial matching start in the next section, when we analyze OPE from higher order vertices.

9.3.2
Cubic vertex and three-point function

In this section, we analyze the (deformed) cubic vertices and the corresponding chiral OPE. We proceed from the easiest cases to the most general case. As in the last section, we omit the Lie algebra indices. They can be easily restored by adding the structure constant
f_{ab}^{c}
to our formula.

flat space action

First, we look at the flat space cubic vertex
I^{(2,0)}_{O}
.

I^{(2,0)}_{O}=\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r,q+s][\mathbf{A}[p,q],\mathbf{A}[r,s]].

(9.25)

This vertex gives us the following tree-level Feynman diagram
I^{(2,0)}_{O}
B[p,q]
B[r,s]
B[p+r,q+s]

The corresponding Feynman integral is the same as the integral in the last section but without
\partial_{z^{\prime}}
derivatives, which gives us the following OPE

B[p,q](z)B[r,s](0)\sim\frac{1}{z}B[p+r,q+s](0).

(9.26)

On the B-brane side, this should be matched with the OPE of a single
I-J
contraction. For example,

(9.27)

Remark 9.2
.

We also have the contraction

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.25346pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 8.88 L 0 13.26 L 0 13.26 L 0 8.88}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})\vbox to13.00002pt{}{}
. However, this term gives us the same combinatorial factor and pole and should be attributed to the Lie algebra factor that we omitted.

For the symmetrized operators, we have

\text{contraction $I-J$ : }\;E^{(p,q)}(z)E^{(r,s)}(0)=\frac{1}{z}E^{(p+r,q+s)}(0)+\dots.

(9.28)

This OPE can also be written as

E^{(n)}(\lambda;z)E^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime};0)\sim\frac{1}{z}\frac{n^{\prime}!}{(n+n^{\prime})!}(\lambda\cdot\partial_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{n}E^{(n+n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime};0)+\dots

(9.29)

where
\lambda\cdot\partial_{\lambda^{\prime}}=\lambda_{1}\partial_{\lambda_{1}^{\prime}}+\lambda_{2}\partial_{\lambda_{2}^{\prime}}
.

We emphasize that there are secretly other terms in this OPE. In fact, after the
I,J
contraction of the symmetrized operators, we obtain
IZ^{(i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n})}Z^{(j_{1}}\dots Z^{j_{n})}J
. This is, in general, not the symmetrized operator
IZ^{(i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n}}Z^{j_{1}}\dots Z^{j_{n})}J
. However, we can always manipulate the final expression into a sum of the symmetrized operator
IZ^{(i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n}}Z^{j_{1}}\dots Z^{j_{n})}J
and other operators in the BRST representative.

For example, an
I,J
contraction of
IZ^{1}J
and
IZ^{2}J
is
IZ^{1}Z^{2}J
, which is not symmetrized. Using the BRST relation, we find that
I[Z^{1},Z^{2}]J
is cohomologous to
IJIJ
. Therefore the term
IZ^{1}Z^{2}J
is cohomologous to
IZ^{(1}Z^{2)}J+\frac{1}{2}IJIJ
. The remaining terms like
IJIJ
are also important, and we will come back to them in the next section.

first order deformed action

We can also consider the deformed cubic vertices. As a simple example, we consider the first order deformation
I_{O}^{(2,1)}
in Section
8.4
. Up to a total derivative, this interaction vertex is given by

\displaystyle I_{O}^{(2,1)}=

\displaystyle N\sum\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q)(r+s)}\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r-1,q+s-1][\partial_{z}\mathbf{A}[p,q],\mathbf{A}[r,s]]

(9.30)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N\sum\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q+r+s)}\frac{1}{r+s}\int\Tr\partial_{z}\mathbf{B}[p+r-1,q+s-1][\mathbf{A}[p,q],\mathbf{A}[r,s]].

The corresponding tree-level diagram gives us the following OPE

\displaystyle B[p,q](z)B[r,s](0)\sim

\displaystyle\frac{N}{z^{2}}\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q)(r+s)}B[p+r-1,q+s-1](0)

(9.31)

\displaystyle+\frac{N}{z}\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q+r+s)}\frac{1}{r+s}\partial_{z}B[p+r-1,q+s-1](0).

This should be matched with the OPE of a single
I-J
contraction together with a
Z-Z
contraction next to the
I-J
contraction. We have

(I(z)Z(\lambda;z)\dots\hbox to7.54pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.77083pt\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.77083pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\lambda;z)\hbox to6.51pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.25346pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.59027pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 8.88 L 0 13.26 L 0 13.26 L 0 8.88}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(0)\hbox to7.54pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.77083pt\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.77083pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 8.88 L 0 17.41 L 0 17.41 L 0 8.88}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\lambda^{\prime},0)\dots J(0))\vbox to16.00002pt{}{}=\frac{N[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]}{z^{2}}I(z)Z(\lambda,z)^{n-1}Z(\lambda^{\prime},0)^{n^{\prime}-1}J(0).

(9.32)

Using BRST relation to symmetrize the operators and drop operators in other BRST representatives, we get

\displaystyle E^{(n)}(\lambda;z)E^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime};0)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{N[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]}{z^{2}}\frac{(n^{\prime}-1)!}{(n+n^{\prime}-2)!}(\lambda\cdot\partial_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{n-1}E^{(n+n^{\prime}-2)}(\lambda^{\prime};0)

(9.33)

\displaystyle+\frac{N[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]}{z}\frac{n}{n+n^{\prime}}\frac{(n^{\prime}-1)!}{(n+n^{\prime}-2)!}(\lambda\cdot\partial_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{n-1}\partial_{z}E^{(n+n^{\prime}-2)}(\lambda^{\prime};0)+\dots

We can rewrite the above OPE using the symmetrized operator
E^{(p,q)}(z)
. The coefficient for the
\frac{1}{z^{2}}E
term can be computed by

\frac{\binom{p+q-1}{q}\binom{r+s-1}{r}-\binom{p+q-1}{p}\binom{r+s-1}{s}}{\binom{p+q}{p}\binom{r+s}{s}}=\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q)(r+s)}.

(9.34)

The coefficient for the
\frac{1}{z}\partial_{z}E
term have an extra
\frac{n}{n+n^{\prime}}=\frac{p+q}{p+q+r+s}
factor. Therefore, the corresponding OPE is given by

\displaystyle E^{(p,q)}(z)E^{(r,s)}(0)\sim

\displaystyle\frac{N}{z^{2}}\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q)(r+s)}E^{(p+r-1,q+s-1)}(0)

(9.35)

\displaystyle+\frac{N}{z}\frac{ps-qr}{(p+q+r+s)}\frac{1}{r+s}\partial_{z}E^{(p+r-1,q+s-1)}(0).

all orders deformed action

Computation of the OPE corresponding to the higher order deformed action is similar. Recall from Section
8.4
that the
k-
th order deformed cubic action
I^{(2,k)}_{O}
takes the following form

\displaystyle I^{(2,k)}_{O}=

\displaystyle N^{k}\sum_{p,q,r,s}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\frac{(-1)^{k_{2}}R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k+1]_{k}}

(9.36)

\displaystyle\times\int\Tr\mathbf{B}[p+r-k,q+s-k][\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\mathbf{A}[p,q],\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{A}[r,s]].

In this case, we have
\partial_{z}
derivatives of total order
k
. The Feynman integral contribute a constant
k!
factor and a pole
\frac{1}{z^{k+1}}
. Therefore, the constant coefficient of the leading term
\frac{N^{k}}{z^{k+1}}B
is given by

k!\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\frac{R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k+1]_{k}}=\frac{R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{[p+q]_{k}[r+s]_{k}},

(9.37)

where we used the formula
E.10
derived in the Appendix. This give the OPE

B[p,q](z)B[r,s](0)\sim\frac{N^{k}}{z^{k+1}}\frac{R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{[p+q]_{k}[r+s]_{k}}B[p+r-k,q+s-k](0)+\dots

(9.38)

To compute other terms with derivatives of
B
, we move the
\partial_{z}
derivatives from one
\mathbf{A}
field to the other
\mathbf{A}
field and the
\mathbf{B}
field in the above cubic action. For example,
\mathbf{B}[\partial_{z}^{k_{1}}\mathbf{A},\partial_{z}^{k_{2}}\mathbf{A}]
can be rewritten as
\sum_{l}(-1)^{k_{2}}\binom{k_{2}}{l}\partial_{z}^{l}\mathbf{B}[\partial_{z}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-l}\mathbf{A},\mathbf{A}]
up to a total
\partial_{z}
derivatives. We see that the term with
\partial_{z}^{l}B
has pole
\frac{1}{z^{k-l+1}}
. The corresponding coefficient is computed by

\displaystyle(k-l)!\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\binom{k_{2}}{l}\frac{R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{k_{1}!k_{2}![p+q]_{k_{1}}[r+s]_{k_{2}}[p+q+r+s-k+1]_{k}}

(9.39)

\displaystyle=\frac{R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{l![p+q]_{k-l}[r+s]_{k}[p+q+r+s-2k+l+1]_{l}},

where we used the formula
E.12
. The full OPE corresponding to the deformed cubic action
I^{(2,k)}_{O}
is thus given by

B[p,q](z)B[r,s](0)\sim\sum_{l=0}^{k}\frac{N^{k}}{z^{k-l+1}}\frac{R_{k}(p,q,r,s)\times\partial_{z}^{l}B[p+r-k,q+s-k](0)}{l![p+q]_{k-l}[r+s]_{k}[p+q+r+s-2k+l+1]_{l}}.

(9.40)

On the B brane side, this OPE should correspond to a single
I-J
contraction together with
k
adjacent
Z-Z
contractions. Such contraction produce a
N^{k}
factor and a pole
\frac{1}{z^{k+1}}
. We have

(I(z)...\hbox to7.54pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.77083pt\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.77083pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 8.88 L 0 21.56 L 0 21.56 L 0 8.88}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\lambda^{\prime};0)...J(0))\vbox to19.00002pt{}{}=\frac{N^{k}}{z^{k+1}}I(z)Z(\lambda;z)^{n-k}Z(\lambda^{\prime};0)^{n^{\prime}-k}J(0).

(9.41)

By symmetrizing the
Z
operators, we can rewrite this OPE as follows

E^{(n)}(\lambda;z)E^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime};0)\sim\frac{N^{k}[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]^{k}}{z^{k+1}}\frac{(n^{\prime}-k)!}{(n+n^{\prime}-2k)!}(\lambda\cdot\partial_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{n-k}E^{(n+n^{\prime}-2k)}(\lambda^{\prime};0)\dots,

(9.42)

where we omitted terms with derivatives of
E
. Expanding the operators into
E^{(p,q)}
, we can compute the constant coefficient by

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\frac{(-1)^{i}\binom{k}{i}\binom{p+q-k}{q-i}\binom{r+s-k}{r-i}}{\binom{p+q}{p}\binom{r+s}{r}}=\frac{R_{k}(p,q,r,s)}{[p+q]_{k}[r+s]_{k}},

(9.43)

which follows from the definition
8.33
of the constant
R_{k}(p,q,r,s)
. This matches the leading term of
9.40
. To obtain the coefficients of the derivative terms, we use the planar three point function

\langle E^{(n)}(\lambda,z)E^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime},z^{\prime})E^{(n^{\prime\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\rangle=\frac{N^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}}{2}+1}[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}}{2}}[\lambda^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime\prime}]^{\frac{n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}-n}{2}}[\lambda\lambda^{\prime\prime}]^{\frac{n+n^{\prime\prime}-n^{\prime}}{2}}}{(z-z^{\prime})^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}}{2}+1}(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime})^{\frac{n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}-n^{\prime}}{2}+1}(z-z^{\prime\prime})^{\frac{n+n^{\prime\prime}-n^{\prime}}{2}+1}},

(9.44)

which is computed via a direct computation of wick contraction. Expanding this three point function we find that

\displaystyle\langle E^{(n)}(\lambda,z)E^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime},z^{\prime})E^{(n^{\prime\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\rangle

(9.45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}}{2}+1}[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}}{2}}[\lambda^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime\prime}]^{\frac{n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}-n}{2}}[\lambda\lambda^{\prime\prime}]^{\frac{n+n^{\prime\prime}-n^{\prime}}{2}}}{(z-z^{\prime})^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}}{2}+1}}\sum_{l\geq 0}\frac{(\frac{n+n^{\prime\prime}-n^{\prime}}{2}+l)!}{(\frac{n+n^{\prime\prime}-n^{\prime}}{2})!l!}(-1)^{l}\left(\frac{z-z^{\prime}}{z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime}}\right)^{l}\frac{1}{(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime})^{n^{\prime\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq 0}\frac{N^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}}{2}+1}[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}}{2}}}{(z-z^{\prime})^{\frac{n+n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}}{2}-l+1}}\frac{[\frac{n+n^{\prime\prime}-n^{\prime}}{2}+l]_{l}(\frac{n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}-n}{2})!}{[n^{\prime\prime}+l+1]_{l}n^{\prime\prime}!}(\lambda\cdot\partial_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{\frac{n+n^{\prime\prime}-n^{\prime}}{2}}[\lambda^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime\prime}]^{n^{\prime\prime}}\frac{\partial_{z^{\prime}}^{l}}{l!}\frac{1}{(z^{\prime}-z^{\prime\prime})^{n^{\prime\prime}+2}}

Using the two-point function, we can extract from the above expression the following OPE

E^{(n)}(\lambda;z)E^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime};0)\sim\sum_{l=0}^{k}\frac{N^{k}[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]^{k}}{z^{k-l+1}}\frac{[n-k+l]_{l}(n^{\prime}-k)!}{[n+n^{\prime}-2k+l+1]_{l}(n+n^{\prime}-2k)!}(\lambda\cdot\partial_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{n-k}\frac{\partial_{z}^{l}}{l!}E^{(n+n^{\prime}-2k)}(\lambda^{\prime};0).

(9.46)

We can check that we get exactly the OPE of the boundary chiral algebra
9.40
.

9.3.3
Quartic vertex

The quartic vertex is constructed using the higher product
m_{3}
that we studied in Section
4.3
. We have the following Feynman diagram.

I^{(3,0)}
B[p,q]
B[r,s]
B[u_{1},v_{1}]
B[u_{2},v_{2}]

The corresponding OPE is given by

B_{a}[p,q](z)B_{b}[r,s](0)\sim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1}+u_{2}=p+r-1\\
v_{1}+v_{2}=q+s-1\end{subarray}}\sum_{c,d,e,f}\frac{K^{fc}}{z}\left((m_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u_{1},v_{1}}f_{bf}^{e}f_{ae}^{d}-(m_{3})^{r,s;p,q}_{u_{1},v_{1}}f_{af}^{e}f_{be}^{d}\right)B_{c}[u_{1},v_{1}]B_{d}[u_{2},v_{2}](0).

(9.47)

where we used the shorthand
(m_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u,v}:=(m_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u,v;p+r-1-u,q+s-1-v}
. We included the Lie algebra factor here since it becomes important in the analysis. Though the above formula is for general Lie algebra, in the holographic setting we need to use the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{gl}(K|K)
or
\mathfrak{gl}(K)
. Strictly speaking, the bulk anomaly of the open-closed coupled theory cancels only for the super Lie algebra
\mathfrak{gl}(K|K)
. However, the OPE matching at this level also works for
\mathfrak{gl}(K)
. For simplicity, we use
\mathfrak{gl}(K)
here. The result can be easily generalized to
\mathfrak{gl}(K|K)
by taking care of the
\pm
sign. Note that
\mathfrak{gl}(K)
has a canonical basis given by the elementary matrices
\{E_{a_{1}a_{2}}\}_{1\leq a_{1},a_{2}\leq K}
. Therefore we replace the indices
a
by
a_{1}a_{2}
in the above formula. The Killing form
K^{a_{1}a_{2};b_{1}b_{2}}
is given by

K^{a_{1}a_{2};b_{1}b_{2}}=\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}.

(9.48)

The structure constant can be extracted from the commutation relation

[E_{a_{1}a_{2}},E_{b_{1}b_{2}}]=\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}E_{a_{1}b_{2}}-\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}E_{b_{1}a_{2}}.

(9.49)

Then the formula
9.47
can be expanded as

\displaystyle B_{a_{1}a_{2}}[p,q](z)

\displaystyle B_{b_{1}b_{2}}[r,s](0)\sim\frac{1}{z}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1}+u_{2}=p+r-1\\
v_{1}+v_{2}=q+s-1\end{subarray}}((m_{3})^{r,s;p,q}_{u_{1},v_{1}}-(m_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u_{1},v_{1}})B_{a_{1}b_{2}}[u_{1},v_{1}]B_{b_{1}a_{2}}[u_{2},v_{2}](0)

(9.50)

\displaystyle+\sum_{c}\left((m_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u_{1},v_{1}}\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}B_{b_{1}c}[u_{1},v_{1}]B_{ca_{2}}[u_{2},v_{2}]-(m_{3})^{r,s;p,q}_{u_{1},v_{1}}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}B_{a_{1}c}[u_{1},v_{1}]B_{cb_{2}}[u_{2},v_{2}]\right).

Naively, we expect that the corresponding wick contraction on the B-brane side is given by a single
Z-Z
contraction

(I...\hbox to7.54pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.77083pt\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.77083pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{i_{k}}...J)(z)(I...\hbox to7.54pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.77083pt\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.77083pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 8.88 L 0 13.26 L 0 13.26 L 0 8.88}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{j_{l}}...J)(0)\vbox to13.00002pt{}{}=\frac{1}{z}(IZ^{j_{1}}...Z^{j_{l-1}}Z^{i_{k}+1}...J)(0)(IZ^{i_{1}}...Z^{i_{k-1}}Z^{j_{l+1}}Z^{j_{1}}...J)(0).

(9.51)

However, as we have mentioned in the last section, after a single
I-J
contraction, we need to manipulate the final expression into BRST representative. In this process, we can trade the commutator
[Z^{1},Z^{1}]
with
\sum J_{a}I_{a}
. Therefore, OPE of the form
(I\dots J)(z)(I\dots J)(0)\sim\frac{1}{z}(I\dots J)(I\dots J)
can also be generated in the single
I-J
wick contraction studied in the last section.

It is a tedious work to analyze the combinatorial factor of the wick contractions in the general situation. To simplify the discussion, we study some special cases.

The simplest example we can look at is the OPE of
I_{a_{1}}Z^{1}J_{a_{2}}
and
I_{b_{1}}Z^{2}J_{b_{2}}
. A single
Z-Z
contraction gives us

(I_{a_{1}}\hbox to7.54pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.77083pt\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.77083pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{1}J_{a_{2}})(z)(I_{b_{1}}\hbox to7.54pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.77083pt\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.77083pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 8.88 L 0 13.26 L 0 13.26 L 0 8.88}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{2}J_{b_{2}})(0)\vbox to13.00002pt{}{}\sim\frac{1}{z}(I_{a_{1}}J_{b_{2}})(I_{b_{1}}J_{a_{2}})(0).

(9.52)

A single
I-J
contraction gives us

\displaystyle(I_{a_{1}}Z^{1}\hbox to6.51pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.25346pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{a_{2}})(z)(\hbox to5.18pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\>\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.59027pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{

{}{}{

{}{}{

{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 8.88 L 0 13.26 L 0 13.26 L 0 8.88}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{b_{1}}Z^{2}J_{b_{2}})(0)\vbox to13.00002pt{}{}

\displaystyle+(\hbox to5.18pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\>\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.59027pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{a_{1}}Z^{1}J_{a_{2}})(z)(I_{b_{1}}Z^{2}\hbox to6.51pt{\vbox to6.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.41666pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.25346pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 8.88 L 0 13.26 L 0 13.26 L 0 8.88}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{b_{2}})(0)\vbox to13.00002pt{}{}

(9.53)

\displaystyle\sim-\frac{1}{2z}\sum_{c}(\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}(I_{a_{1}}J_{c})(I_{c}J_{b_{2}})(0)+\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}(I_{b_{1}}J_{c})(I_{c}J_{a_{2}})),

where we omitted the operator
IZ^{(1}Z^{2)}J
that analyzed in the last section.

By computing
m_{3}
using
4.39
, we find that
(m_{3})^{1,0;0,1}_{0,0}=-\frac{1}{2}
and
(m_{3})^{0,1;1,0}_{0,0}=\frac{1}{2}
. Therefore we have the following OPE

\displaystyle B_{a_{1}a_{2}}[1,0](z)B_{b_{1}b_{2}}[0,1](0)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{z}\Bigl(B_{a_{1}b_{2}}[0,0]B_{b_{1}a_{2}}[0,0](0)

(9.54)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{c}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}B_{a_{1}c}[0,0]B_{cb_{2}}[0,0](0)-\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}B_{b_{1}c}[0,0]B_{ca_{2}}[0,0](0)\Bigr).

This matches exactly the corresponding OPE on B-brane.

As a more nontrivial example, we use the formula
4.39
to compute the following value of
m_{3}

\displaystyle(m_{3})^{1,0;r,s}_{u,v}

\displaystyle=-\frac{r!s!(u+v+1)!(r+s-u-v-1)!}{(r+s+1)!u!v!(r-u)!(s-v-1)!},

(9.55)

\displaystyle(m_{3})^{r,s;1,0}_{u,v}

\displaystyle=\frac{r!s!(u+v)!(r+s-u-v)!}{(r+s+1)!u!v!(r-u)!(s-v-1)!}.

Therefore, we have the following OPE

\displaystyle B_{a_{1}a_{2}}[1,0](z)B_{b_{1}b_{2}}[r,s](0)

(9.56)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z}\sum_{u,v}\binom{r+s}{r}^{-1}\binom{u+v}{u}\binom{r+s-u-v-1}{r-u}\Bigl(B_{a_{1}b_{2}}[u,v]B_{b_{1}a_{2}}[r-u,s-v-1](0)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{c}\frac{r+s-u-v}{r+s+1}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}B_{a_{1}c}[u,v]B_{cb_{2}}[r-u,s-v-1](0)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{c}\frac{u+v}{r+s+1}\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}B_{b_{1}c}[u,v]B_{ca_{2}}[r-u,s-v-1](0)\Bigr).

On the B-brane side, we can compute a single
Z-Z
contraction as follows

E_{a_{1}a_{2}}^{(1)}(\lambda;z)E_{b_{1}b_{2}}^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime},0)\sim\sum_{n^{\prime\prime}}\frac{[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]}{z}E^{(n^{\prime\prime})}_{a_{1}b_{2}}(\lambda^{\prime},0)E^{(n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}-1)}_{b_{1}a_{2}}(\lambda^{\prime},0).

(9.57)

Expanding the above formula with
E_{b_{1}b_{2}}^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime},0)=\sum_{r+s=n^{\prime}}\binom{r+s}{r}E_{b_{1}b_{2}}^{(r,s)}(0)\lambda_{1}^{\prime r}\lambda_{2}^{\prime s}
, we find

E_{a_{1}a_{2}}^{(1,0)}(z)B_{b_{1}b_{2}}^{(r,s)}(0)\sim\frac{1}{z}\sum_{u,v}\binom{r+s}{r}^{-1}\binom{u+v}{u}\binom{r+s-u-v-1}{r-u}E_{a_{1}b_{2}}^{(u,v)}B_{b_{1}a_{2}}^{(r-u,s-v-1)}(0).

(9.58)

This matches with the first term of the boundary OPE of
9.56
. To match the remaining terms, we consider the single
I-J
contraction. As in previous discussion, we use the BRST relation and find that
I\dots[Z(\lambda),Z(\lambda^{\prime})]\dots J
is cohomologous to
[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]I\dots JI\dots J
. Therefore, after dropping the symmetrized operator that is already studied in the last section, we have the following OPE

\displaystyle E_{a_{1}a_{2}}^{(1)}(\lambda;z)E_{b_{1}b_{2}}^{(n^{\prime})}(\lambda^{\prime},0)

\displaystyle\sim-\frac{[\lambda\lambda^{\prime}]}{z}\sum_{n^{\prime\prime}}\sum_{c}\left(\frac{n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}}{n^{\prime}+1}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}E^{(n^{\prime\prime})}_{a_{1}c}(\lambda^{\prime},0)E^{(n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}-1)}_{cb_{2}}(\lambda^{\prime},0)\right.

(9.59)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{n^{\prime\prime}+1}{n^{\prime}+1}\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}E^{(n^{\prime\prime})}_{b_{1}c}(\lambda^{\prime},0)E^{(n^{\prime}-n^{\prime\prime}-1)}_{ca_{2}}(\lambda^{\prime},0)\right).

Expanding this formula, we find

\displaystyle E_{a_{1}a_{2}}^{(1,0)}(z)B_{b_{1}b_{2}}^{(r,s)}(0)\sim-\frac{1}{z}\sum_{u,v}\sum_{c}\binom{r+s}{r}^{-1}\binom{u+v}{u}\binom{r+s-u-v-1}{r-u}

(9.60)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{r+s-u-v}{r+s+1}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}E_{a_{1}c}^{(u,v)}B_{cb_{2}}^{(r-u,s-v-1)}(0)+\frac{u+v+1}{r+s+1}\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}E_{b_{1}c}^{(u,v)}B_{ca_{2}}^{(r-u,s-v-1)}(0)\right).

This gives the remaining terms of the boundary OPE of
9.56
.

As we have discussed, the deformed geometry will introduce deformation to the higher operations and gives us deformed interaction vertices. We expect that the deformed quartic action
I^{(3,k)}_{O}
corresponds to the wick contraction of
k+1
adjacent
Z-Z
fields, and also the wick contraction of a single
I-J
together with
k
adjacent
Z-Z
fields. It will be interesting to explore these OPE.

9.4
Comments on higher order interaction

In this section we briefly analyze OPE coming from higher order vertices. First, we study the quintic interaction vertices. By looking at the Lie algebra factor, we see that this interaction term produce the following terms in the OPE of
B_{a_{1}a_{2}}(z)B_{b_{1}b_{2}}(0)

\displaystyle\sum_{c,c^{\prime}}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}B_{a_{1}c}B_{cc^{\prime}}B_{c^{\prime}b_{2}}

\displaystyle,\quad\sum_{c,c^{\prime}}\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}B_{b_{1}c}B_{cc^{\prime}}B_{c^{\prime}a_{2}},

(9.61)

\displaystyle\sum_{c}B_{a_{1}c}B_{cb_{2}}B_{b_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle,\quad\sum_{c}B_{a_{1}b_{2}}B_{b_{1}c}B_{ca_{2}}.

Naively, wick contraction of two
IZ\dots J
operators cannot produce operators with three or more pairs of
I,J
fields. However, as we have analyzed in the last section, we trade the commutator
[Z^{1},Z^{2}]
with
\sum_{c}J_{c}I_{c}
in this process of symmetrize the adjoint fields. This will gives OPE that produce three or more pairs of
I,J
fields as desired. For example, a single
Z-Z
contraction of
I_{a_{1}}\dots J_{a_{2}}
and
I_{b_{1}}\dots J_{b_{2}}
gives
I_{a_{1}}\dots J_{b_{2}}I_{b_{1}}\dots J_{a_{2}}
. Symmetrizing this operator gives the following two possibilities with three pairs of
I,J
fields.

\sum_{c}(I_{a_{1}}\dots J_{c})(I_{c}\dots J_{b_{2}})(I_{b_{1}}\dots J_{a_{2}}),\quad\sum_{c}(I_{a_{1}}\dots J_{b_{2}})(I_{b_{1}}\dots J_{c})(I_{c}\dots J_{a_{2}}).

(9.62)

They match the operators in the second line of
9.61
. We can also consider a single
I-J
contraction. This will gives us the operators
\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}I_{a_{1}}\dots J_{b_{2}}
and
\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}I_{b_{1}}\dots J_{a_{2}}
. To symmetrize this operator we need to use
[Z^{1},Z^{2}]\sim\sum_{c}J_{c}I_{c}
more than once in general. This gives the following two possibilities with three pairs of
I,J
fields.

\sum_{c,c^{\prime}}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}(I_{a_{1}}\dots J_{c})(I_{c}\dots J_{c^{\prime}})(I_{c^{\prime}}\dots J_{b_{2}}),\quad\sum_{c,c^{\prime}}\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}(I_{b_{1}}\dots J_{c})(I_{c}\dots J_{c^{\prime}})(I_{c^{\prime}}\dots J_{a_{2}}).

(9.63)

They match the operators in the first line of
9.61
. So far, we have only considered the matching of Lie algebra indices in the OPE. It will be a nontrivial and interesting prediction of twisted holography that the
A_{\infty}
structure on the Cauchy-Riemann cohomology can reproduce the combinatorial factor coming from matrix contraction and symmetrization of the operators.

More generally, we expect that the full tree-level OPE for the KK theory on deformed geometry takes the following schematic form

\displaystyle B_{a_{1}a_{2}}[p,q](z)B_{b_{1}b_{2}}[r,s](0)\sim\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1}+\dots u_{n}=p+r-k-n+1\\
v_{1}+\dots v_{n}=q+s-k-n+1\end{subarray}}\frac{N^{k}}{z^{k+1}}\times(\text{some constants})\times

(9.64)

\displaystyle\sum_{c_{1},\dots,c_{n-1}}\delta_{a_{2}b_{1}}B_{a_{1}c_{1}}[u_{1},v_{1}]B_{c_{1}c_{2}}[u_{2},v_{2}]\dots B_{c_{n-1}b_{2}}[u_{n},v_{n}]+\delta_{a_{1}b_{2}}B_{b_{1}c_{1}}[u_{1},v_{1}]B_{c_{1}c_{2}}[u_{2},v_{2}]\dots B_{c_{n-1}a_{2}}[u_{n},v_{n}]

\displaystyle\sum_{c_{1},\dots,c_{n-2}}\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}B_{a_{1}c_{1}}[u_{1},v_{1}]B_{c_{1}c_{2}}[u_{2},v_{2}]\dots B_{c_{l-1}b_{2}}[u_{l},v_{l}]B_{b_{1}c_{l}}[u_{l+1},v_{l+1}]B_{c_{1}c_{l+1}}[u_{l+2},v_{l+2}]\dots B_{c_{n-2}a_{2}}[u_{n},v_{n}]

\displaystyle+\text{derivatives}.

Under the normalization
B[p,q]\to N^{-\frac{p+q+2}{2}}B[p,q]
, we find that the above OPE’s are all of order
\frac{1}{N}
. Dropping the
\frac{1}{N^{2}}
and higher terms in the full OPE provides us with an interesting limit of the holography chiral algebra beyond the planar limit. In fact, this limit gives us a Poisson vertex algebra. Matching this Poisson vertex algebra structure is a very nontrivial but also approachable test of the twisted holography.

9.5
Chiral algebra from abelian Chern-Simons

As explained in
[
7
]
, the
\mathrm{PV}^{2,0}
and
\mathrm{PV}^{0,2}
part of Kodaira-Spencer theory can be reformulated as a super abelian Chern-Simons theory valued in
\Pi\mathbb{C}^{2}

8
8

8

This notation simply means the abelian Lie super algebra of
2
-dimension in degree
1
.
. This abelian Lie algebra has bilinear pairing given by the symplectic form on
\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Therefore, we can directly apply our previous results about the holomorphic Chern-Simons theory. After the KK reduction, we have the following field content

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\in\Omega_{3d}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}\otimes\Pi\mathbb{C}^{2}[w_{1},w_{2}][1],

(9.65)

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\in\Omega_{3d}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}},(1)}\otimes\Pi\mathbb{C}^{2}[\bar{w}_{,}\bar{w}_{2}].

Since
\Pi\mathbb{C}^{2}
is an abelian algebra, all brackets and higher brackets
l_{k}
vanish for this theory. The only nontrivial terms are the quadratic action and its deformation discussed in
8.2
. We immediately obtain the action functional for this theory

\sum_{p,q}\int\epsilon_{ab}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}_{a}[p,q]\hat{d}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{b}[p,q]+\frac{(-1)^{q}N^{p+q+1}}{(p+q+1)!}\frac{p!q!}{(p+q)!}\int\epsilon_{ab}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{a}[p,q]\partial_{z}^{p+q+1}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{b}[q,p].

(9.66)

Taking the Dirichlet boundary condition, the boundary chiral algebra is generated by

\partial_{z}^{k}B^{\prime}_{a}[p,q],\;\;a=1,2.

(9.67)

As we have discussed, the quadratic term gives the following OPE

B^{\prime}_{1}[p,q](z)B^{\prime}_{2}[q,p](0)\sim(-1)^{q}N^{p+q+1}\frac{p!q!}{(p+q)!}\frac{1}{z^{p+q+2}}.

(9.68)

On the B brane side, this part of boundary algebra should be matched with the
B^{(n)}
and
C^{(n)}
towers of fields.

\displaystyle\Tr(bZ^{(i_{1}}Z^{i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n})}),

(9.69)

\displaystyle\Tr(\partial cZ^{(i_{1}}Z^{i_{1}}\dots Z^{i_{n})}).

The corresponding OPE is given by the full wick contraction as follows
