{{{}{}{}{}}}
{{{}{}{}{}}}
{{}{}{}{}}}}{}
{}
{}
{}
{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 12.6 C -5.44 22.01 -17.48 25.24 -26.89 19.8 C -36.31 14.37 -39.53 2.33 -34.1 -7.09 C -28.66 -16.5 -16.62 -19.73 -7.21 -14.29 C -4.21 -12.56 -1.73 -10.08 -0.28 -7.57}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.50003}{0.86601}{-0.86601}{0.50003}{-0.09999pt}{-5.2945pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\bar{S}^{c}(sL),Sd^{s})\overset{Sp^{s}}{\underset{Si^{s}}{\rightleftarrows}}(\bar{S}^{c}(s\mathfrak{g}),0),

(C.33)

where the differential
Sd^{s}
is defined by
Sd^{s}=\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathds{1}^{i}\otimes(s\circ d\circ s^{-1})\otimes\mathds{1}^{n-i-1}
. The projection and inclusion maps are defined by
Sp^{s}=\sum_{n\geq 1}(s\circ p\circ s^{-1})^{\otimes n}
and
Si^{s}=\sum_{n\geq 1}(s\circ i\circ s^{-1})^{\otimes n}
. The deformation retract is defined as

Sh^{s}=\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n}}\sigma^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathds{1}^{\otimes i}\otimes(s\circ h\circ s^{-1})\otimes(s\circ i\circ p\circ s^{-1})^{\otimes n-i-1}\right)\sigma.

The Lie bracket
[-,-]
on
L
defined a map
Q_{2}:(sL)^{\otimes 2}\to sL
and extend to a map
\delta:\bar{S}^{c}(sL)\to\bar{T}^{c}(sL)
. We add this differential to the above SDR as a perturbation. Then we have a new SDR, with a new differential on
\bar{S}^{c}(s\mathfrak{g})
given by the following

Q^{\prime}=Sp^{s}\circ(1-\delta\circ Sh^{s})^{-1}\circ\delta\circ Si^{s}=\sum_{n\geq 0}Sp^{s}\circ(\delta\circ Sh^{s})^{n}\circ\delta\circ Si^{s}.

(C.34)

We can expand the above formula into components. This gives us the usual tree description of the transferred
L_{\infty}
structure on
\mathfrak{g}
. Let
\mathrm{BT_{n}}
be the set of binary rooted trees with
n
leaves. In this case, we need to consider trees not necessarily planar, which means edges can cross each other. We consider the following construction that assigns each
T\in\mathrm{BT}_{n}
an
n
array operation
l_{T}
on
H
. The operation
l_{T}
is obtained by putting
i
on the leaves,
[-,-]
on the vertices,
h
on the internal edges and
p
on the root. We consider

l_{n}=\sum_{T\in BT_{n}}(\pm)l_{T}.

(C.35)

Then the operations
\{l_{n}\}_{n\geq 2}
defined an
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on
\mathfrak{g}
. Moreover, the
L_{\infty}
algebra
(\mathfrak{g},l_{2},l_{3},\dots)
is
L_{\infty}
quasi-isomorphic to the dg Lie algebra
(L,d,[-,-])
.

Appendix D
Computation of (higher) products and brackets

D.1
Product of
S^{3}
harmonics

In this section, we compute the product of two arbitrary
S^{3}
harmonics. We first recall the formula
4.28
that decomposes a harmonic polynomial into sum of monomials

e^{(j,\bar{j})}_{m}=\sum_{l}\lambda_{j,\bar{j},0}^{-1}C^{j,\bar{j};j+\bar{j}}_{m-l,l;m}e^{(j)}_{m-l}\bar{e}^{\bar{j}}_{l},

(D.1)

where

\lambda_{j,\bar{j},k}=(-1)^{k}\sqrt{\frac{(2j+1)!(2\bar{j}+1)!}{k!(2j+2\bar{j}-k+1)!}}.

(D.2)

Then we can write

M(e^{(j_{1},\bar{j}_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2},\bar{j}_{2})}_{m_{2}})=\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1},0}^{-1}\lambda_{j_{2},\bar{j}_{2},0}^{-1}C^{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1};j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}}_{m_{1}-l_{1},l_{1};m_{1}}C^{j_{2},\bar{j}_{2};j_{2}+\bar{j}_{2}}_{m_{2}-l_{2},l_{2};m_{2}}M(e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}-l_{1}}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{1})}_{l_{1}},e^{(j_{2})}_{m_{2}-l_{2}}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{2})}_{l_{2}}).

(D.3)

To compute
M(e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}-l_{1}}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{1})}_{l_{1}},e^{(j_{2})}_{m_{2}-l_{2}}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{2})}_{l_{2}})
, we consider the product
e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}-l_{1}}e^{(j_{2})}_{m_{2}-l_{2}}
and
\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{1})}_{l_{1}}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{2})}_{l_{2}}
separately. We find

\displaystyle M(e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}-l_{1}}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{1})}_{l_{1}},e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}-l}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{1})}_{l_{2}})

(D.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(2j_{1}+1)(2j_{2}+1)(2\bar{j}_{1}+1)(2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}{(2j_{1}+2j_{2}+1)(2\bar{j}_{1}+2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}}C^{j_{1},j_{2};j_{1}+j_{2}}_{m_{1}-l_{1},m_{2}-l_{2},m_{1}+m_{2}-l_{1}-l_{2}}C^{\bar{j}_{1},\bar{j}_{2};\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}}_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{1}+l_{2}}M(e^{(j_{1}+j_{2})}_{m_{1}+m_{2}-l_{1}-l_{2}},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2})}_{l_{1}+l_{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\lambda_{j_{1}+j_{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2},k}\sqrt{\frac{(2j_{1}+1)(2j_{2}+1)(2\bar{j}_{1}+1)(2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}{(2j_{1}+2j_{2}+1)(2\bar{j}_{1}+2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}}

\displaystyle\times C^{j_{1},j_{2};j_{1}+j_{2}}_{m_{1}-l_{1},m_{2}-l_{2};m_{1}+m_{2}-l_{1}-l_{2}}C^{\bar{j}_{1},\bar{j}_{2};\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}}_{l_{1},l_{2};l_{1}+l_{2}}C^{j_{1}+j_{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2};j_{1}+j_{2}+\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-k}_{m_{1}+m_{2}-l_{1}-l_{2},l_{1}+l_{2};m_{1}+m_{2}}e^{(j_{1}+j_{2}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{m_{1}+m_{2}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle M(e^{(j_{1},\bar{j}_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2},\bar{j}_{2})}_{m_{2}})

(D.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1},0}^{-1}\lambda_{j_{2},\bar{j}_{2},0}^{-1}\lambda_{j_{1}+j_{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2},k}\sqrt{\frac{(2j_{1}+1)(2j_{2}+1)(2\bar{j}_{1}+1)(2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}{(2j_{1}+2j_{2}+1)(2\bar{j}_{1}+2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle C^{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1};j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}}_{m_{1}-l_{1},l_{1};m_{1}}C^{j_{2},\bar{j}_{2};j_{2}+\bar{j}_{2}}_{m_{2}-l_{2},l_{2};m_{2}}C^{j_{1},j_{2};j_{1}+j_{2}}_{m_{1}-l_{1},m_{2}-l_{2};m_{1}+m_{2}-l_{1}-l_{2}}C^{\bar{j}_{1},\bar{j}_{2};\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}}_{l_{1},l_{2};l_{1}+l_{2}}C^{j_{1}+j_{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2};j_{1}+j_{2}+\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-k}_{m_{1}+m_{2}-l_{1}-l_{2},l_{1}+l_{2};m_{1}+m_{2}}e^{(j_{1}+j_{2}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{m_{1}+m_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1},0}^{-1}\lambda_{j_{2},\bar{j}_{2},0}^{-1}\lambda_{j_{1}+j_{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2},k}\sqrt{(2j_{1}+1)(2j_{2}+1)(2\bar{j}_{1}+1)(2\bar{j}_{2}+1)(2j_{1}+2\bar{j}_{1}+1)(2j_{2}+2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{1}+j_{2}\\
\bar{j}_{1}&\bar{j}_{2}&\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}\\
j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}&j_{2}+\bar{j}_{2}&j_{1}+j_{2}+\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-k\end{Bmatrix}C^{j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1},j_{2}+\bar{j}_{2};j_{1}+j_{2}+\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-k}_{m_{1},m_{2};m_{1}+m_{2}}e^{(j_{1}+j_{2}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{m_{1}+m_{2}},

where
\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}&j_{2}&j_{3}\\
j_{4}&j_{5}&j_{6}\\
j_{7}&j_{8}&j_{9}\end{Bmatrix}
is the Wigner
9-j
symbol.

In our study of the higher product on the CR cohomology, a constantly appearing computation is the product of the form
M(e^{(j_{1}-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}_{1}-\frac{i}{2})}_{m_{1}},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j_{2}})}_{m_{2}})
. One can use the above general formula to compute this. Here, we derive an alternative formula that is more succinct. The key is that we use a variation of
4.28
to expand the harmonics polynomial
e^{(j_{1}-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}_{1}-\frac{i}{2})}_{m_{1}}

e^{(j_{1}-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}_{1}-\frac{i}{2})}_{m_{1}}=\sum_{l}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1},i}^{-1}C^{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1};j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}-i}_{m_{1}-l,l;m_{1}}e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}-l}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{1})}_{l}.

(D.6)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle M(e^{(j_{1}-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}_{1}-\frac{i}{2})}_{m_{1}},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{2})}_{m_{2}})=\sum_{l}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1},i}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{(2\bar{j}_{1}+1)(2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}{(2\bar{j}_{1}+2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}}C^{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1};j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}-i}_{m_{1}-l,l;m_{1}}C^{\bar{j}_{1},\bar{j}_{2};\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}}_{l,m_{2};l+m_{2}}M(e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}-l},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2})}_{l+m_{2}})

(D.7)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{l}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1},i}^{-1}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2};k}\sqrt{\frac{(2\bar{j}_{1}+1)(2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}{(2\bar{j}_{1}+2\bar{j}_{2}+1)}}C^{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1};j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}-i}_{m_{1}-l,l;m_{1}}C^{\bar{j}_{1},\bar{j}_{2};\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}}_{l,m_{2};l+m_{2}}C^{j_{1},\bar{j_{1}}+\bar{j}_{2};j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-k}_{m_{1}-l,m_{2}+l;m_{1}+m_{2}}e^{(j_{1}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{m_{1}+m_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 0}(-1)^{2(j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2})-k}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1},i}^{-1}\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2};k}\sqrt{(2\bar{j}_{1}+1)(2\bar{j}_{2}+1)(2j_{1}+2\bar{j}_{1}-2i+1)}

\displaystyle\times\begin{Bmatrix}\bar{j}_{1}&j_{1}&j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}-i\\
j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-k&\bar{j}_{2}&\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}\end{Bmatrix}C^{j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}-i,\bar{j}_{2};j_{1}+\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-k}_{m_{1},m_{2};m_{1}+m_{2}}e^{(j_{1}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}_{1}+\bar{j}_{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{m_{1}+m_{2}}.

Though we write the summation range as
k\geq 0
, the Wigner
6j
symbol actually constraint it such that
k\geq i
and
k\leq\min\{2j_{1},2\bar{j}_{1}+2\bar{j}_{2}\}
.

D.2
3
-brackets of Poisson BF theory

In this Appendix, we give a general formula for the
3
-bracket in the Poisson BF theory. We compute the constant

\displaystyle(\pi_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u_{1},v_{1},u_{2},v_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(u_{1}+v_{1}+1)!(u_{2}+v_{2}+1)!}{u_{1}!v_{1}!u_{2}!v_{2}!}\times\Tr_{S^{3}}(w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q}\times p\{h\{w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s},\bar{w}_{1}^{u_{1}}\bar{w}_{2}^{v_{1}}\epsilon\},\bar{w}_{1}^{u_{2}}\bar{w}_{2}^{v_{2}}\epsilon\})

(D.8)

\displaystyle=\frac{(u_{1}+v_{1}+1)!(u_{2}+v_{2}+1)!}{u_{1}!v_{1}!u_{2}!v_{2}!}\times\Tr_{S^{3}}(\bar{w}_{1}^{u_{2}}\bar{w}_{2}^{v_{2}}\epsilon\times p\{w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},h\{w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s},\bar{w}_{1}^{u_{1}}\bar{w}_{2}^{v_{1}}\epsilon\}\})

Since
(\pi_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u_{1},v_{1},u_{2},v_{2}}
is only nonzero when
u_{1}+u_{2}=p+r-3,v_{1}+v_{2}=q+s-3
, we denote
(\pi_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u,v}:=(\pi_{3})^{p,q;r,s}_{u,v,p+r-u-3,q+s-v-3}
throughout this section.

First, we need to give a
SU(2)
decomposition of the two bracket
\{-,-\}_{\bar{\pi}}
on the CR complex. From
6.22
, we see that the induced bracket on the CR complex restricted to
\mathcal{H}_{j,0}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{0,\bar{j}}\epsilon
gives a map

\displaystyle\{-,-\}:\mathcal{H}_{j,0}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{0,\bar{j}}\epsilon

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{H}_{j-\frac{1}{2},0}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{0,\bar{j}+\frac{1}{2}}\epsilon

(D.9)

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{H}_{j-\frac{1}{2},\bar{j}+\frac{1}{2}}\epsilon\oplus\mathcal{H}_{j-1,\bar{j}}\epsilon\oplus\dots

We can analyze this map using the same techniques as in Section
4.2
.

\displaystyle\{-,-\}\circ CG^{-1}(e^{(j+\bar{j}-k)}_{j+\bar{j}-k}\epsilon)

(D.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}(-1)^{k}C^{j,\bar{j};j+\bar{j}-k}_{j,\bar{j}-k;j+\bar{j}-k}\frac{k!}{(k-l)!l!}\sqrt{\frac{(2j+1)(2\bar{j}+1)!}{k!(2\bar{j}-k)!}}

\displaystyle\times(2\bar{j}+2)(lw_{1}\bar{w}_{1}-(2j-l)w_{2}\bar{w}_{2})w_{1}^{2j-l-1}w_{2}^{l-1}\bar{w}_{1}^{k-l}\bar{w}_{2}^{2\bar{j}-k+l}\epsilon

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k+1}\sqrt{\frac{(2j+1)!(2\bar{j}+1)!}{k!(2j+2\bar{j}-k+1)!}}\sqrt{(2\bar{j}-k+1)(2j-k)}(2\bar{j}+2)e^{(j-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{j+\bar{j}-k}.

Therefore, the map
\{-,-\}:\mathcal{H}_{j,0}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{0,\bar{j}}\epsilon\to\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\min\{2j-1,2\bar{j}+1\}}\mathcal{H}_{j-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2}}\epsilon
is given by

\{e^{(j)}_{m},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j})}_{\bar{m}}\epsilon\}=\sum_{k}\tilde{\lambda}_{j,\bar{j},k}C^{j,\bar{j};j+\bar{j}-k}_{m,\bar{m};m+\bar{m}}e^{(j-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{m+\bar{m}}\epsilon,

(D.11)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}_{j,\bar{j},k}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k+1}\sqrt{\frac{(2j+1)!(2\bar{j}+1)!}{k!(2j+2\bar{j}-k+1)!}}\sqrt{(2\bar{j}-k+1)(2j-k)}(2\bar{j}+2)

(D.12)

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{(2\bar{j}-k+1)(2j-k)}(2\bar{j}+2)\lambda_{j,\bar{j},k}.

We emphasis that the bracket restricted to
\mathcal{H}_{j,0}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{0,\bar{j}}
is different. We have

\{w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},\bar{w}_{1}^{r}\bar{w}_{2}^{s}\}=(r+s)(qw_{1}\bar{w}_{1}-pw_{2}\bar{w}_{2})w_{1}^{p-1}w_{2}^{q-1}\bar{w}_{1}^{r}\bar{w}_{1}^{s}\epsilon.

(D.13)

As a result, we have

\{e^{(j)}_{m},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j})}_{\bar{m}}\}=\sum_{k}-\sqrt{(2\bar{j}-k+1)(2j-k)}(2\bar{j})\lambda_{j,\bar{j},k}C^{j,\bar{j};j+\bar{j}-k}_{m,\bar{m};m+\bar{m}}e^{(j-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{m+\bar{m}}.

(D.14)

To compute the constant
\pi^{p,q;r,s}_{u,v}
we compute the map
p\{w_{1}^{p}w_{2}^{q},h\{w_{1}^{r}w_{2}^{s},\bar{w}_{1}^{u_{1}}\bar{w}_{2}^{v_{1}}\epsilon\}\}
. First, we compute
h\{e^{(j_{2})}_{m_{2}},\bar{e}^{\bar{(j)}}_{\bar{m}}\epsilon\}
. Using
D.11
, we have

h\{e^{(j_{2})}_{m_{2}},\bar{e}^{\bar{(j)}}_{\bar{m}}\epsilon\}=\sum_{i}\tilde{\lambda}_{j_{2},\bar{j},i}h_{j_{2}-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{i}{2}}C^{j_{2},\bar{j};j_{2}+\bar{j}-i}_{m,\bar{m};m_{2}+\bar{m}}e^{(j_{2}-1-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}+1-\frac{i}{2})}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}}.

(D.15)

Then we compute
p\{e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2}-1-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}+1-\frac{i}{2})}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}}\}
. Using
D.7
we have

\displaystyle p\{e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2}-1-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}+1-\frac{i}{2})}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}}\}=\sum_{m^{\prime}}\lambda_{j_{2}-1,\bar{j}+1,i}^{-1}C^{j_{2}-1,\bar{j}+1;j_{2}+\bar{j}-i}_{m^{\prime},m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime};m_{2}+\bar{m}}p\{e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2}-1)}_{m^{\prime}}\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}+1)}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime}}\}

(D.16)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m^{\prime}}\lambda_{j_{2}-1,\bar{j}+1,i}^{-1}C^{j_{2}-1,\bar{j}+1;j_{2}+\bar{j}-i}_{m^{\prime},m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime};m_{2}+\bar{m}}\left(pM(\{e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2}-1)}_{m^{\prime}}\},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}+1)}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime}})+pM(e^{(j_{2}-1)}_{m^{\prime}},\{e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}+1)}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime}}\})\right).

The first term in the above formula is given by

\displaystyle pM(\{e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2}-1)}_{m^{\prime}}\},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}+1)}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime}})

(D.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{2\bar{j}}\sqrt{\frac{[2j_{1}+1]_{2}[2j_{2}-1]_{2}(2j_{1}+2j_{2}-2)(2\bar{j}+3)}{2j_{1}+j_{2}-3}}C^{j_{1},j_{2}-1;j_{1}+j_{2}-2}_{m_{1},m^{\prime};m_{1}+m^{\prime}}C^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2,\bar{j}+1,j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3}_{m_{1}+m^{\prime},m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime};m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}}e^{(j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3)}_{m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}}.

The second term can be computed by

\displaystyle pM(e^{(j_{2}-1)}_{m^{\prime}},\{e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},\bar{e}^{(\bar{j}+1)}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime}}\})

(D.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}-\sqrt{(2\bar{j}-k+3)(2j_{1}-k)}(2\bar{j}+2)\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}+1,k}C^{j_{1},\bar{j}+1,j_{1}+\bar{j}+1-k}_{m_{1},m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime};m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime}}pM(e^{(j_{2}-1)}_{m^{\prime}},e^{(j_{1}-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2},\bar{j}+1+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{k}{2})}_{m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}(-1)^{2(j_{1}+j_{2})-k}\sqrt{(2\bar{j}-k+3)(2j_{1}-k)}(2\bar{j}+2)\lambda_{j_{1},\bar{j}+1,k}\lambda_{j_{1}-\frac{1}{2},\bar{j}+\frac{3}{2},k}^{-1}\sqrt{2j_{1}(2j_{2}+1)(2\bar{j}+4)(2j_{1}+2\bar{j}-2k+3)}

\displaystyle\times\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}-\frac{1}{2}&\bar{j}+\frac{3}{2}&j_{1}+\bar{j}+1-k\\
j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3&j_{2}-1&j_{1}+j_{2}-\frac{3}{2}\end{Bmatrix}C^{j_{1},\bar{j}+1,j_{1}+\bar{j}+1-k}_{m_{1},m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime};m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime}}C^{j_{1}+\bar{j}+1-k,j_{2}-1;j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3}_{m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}-m^{\prime},m^{\prime};m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}}e^{(j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3)}_{m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}}.

We find that

p\{e^{(j_{1})}_{m_{1}},e^{(j_{2}-1-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}+1-\frac{i}{2})}_{m_{2}+\bar{m}}\}=\lambda_{j_{2}-1,\bar{j}+1,i}^{-1}\Pi_{j_{1},j_{2},\bar{j};i}C^{j_{2}+\bar{j}-i,j_{1};j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3}_{m_{2}+\bar{m},m_{1},m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}}e^{(j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3)}_{m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}},

(D.19)

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{j_{1},j_{2},\bar{j};i}=(-1)^{2(j_{1}+j_{2})-i}\sqrt{[2j_{1}+1]_{2}[2j_{2}-1]_{2}(2j_{1}+2j_{2}-2)(2\bar{j}+3)(2j_{1}+2\bar{j}-2i+1)}

(D.20)

\displaystyle\times\begin{Bmatrix}j_{2}-1&\bar{j}+1&j_{2}+\bar{j}-i\\
j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3&j_{1}&j_{1}+j_{2}-2\end{Bmatrix}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k}(-1)^{2(j_{1}+j_{2})-k}\sqrt{[2j_{1}+1]_{2}(2j_{2}+1)(2j_{2}+2\bar{j}-2i+1)(2\bar{j}-k+3)(2j_{1}-k)}(2\bar{j}+2)

\displaystyle\times(2j_{1}+2\bar{j}-2k+3)\begin{Bmatrix}j_{1}-\frac{1}{2}&\bar{j}+\frac{3}{2}&j_{1}+\bar{j}+1-k\\
j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3&j_{2}-1&j_{1}+j_{2}-\frac{3}{2}\end{Bmatrix}\begin{Bmatrix}\bar{j}+1&j_{2}-1&j_{1}+\bar{j}-i\\
j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3&j_{1}&j_{1}+\bar{j}+1-k\end{Bmatrix}.

We have

\displaystyle(\pi_{3})^{j_{1}+m_{1},j_{1}-m_{1};j_{2}+m_{2},j_{2}-m_{2}}_{\bar{j}-\bar{m},\bar{j}+\bar{m}}:=\frac{(-1)^{\bar{j}+\bar{m}}N(j_{1},m_{1})N(j_{2},m_{2})}{N(\bar{j},\bar{m})N(j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3,m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m})}

(D.21)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{i}\tilde{\lambda}_{j_{2},\bar{j},i}\lambda_{j_{2}-1,\bar{j}+1,i}^{-1}h_{j_{2}-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{i}{2},\bar{j}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{i}{2}}\Pi_{j_{1},j_{2},\bar{j};i}C^{j_{2},\bar{j};j_{2}+\bar{j}-i}_{m_{2},\bar{m};m_{2}+\bar{m}}C^{j_{2}+\bar{j}-i,j_{1};j_{1}+j_{2}-\bar{j}-3}_{m_{2}+\bar{m},m_{1},m_{1}+m_{2}+\bar{m}}.

This constant gives the quartic interaction of the KK theory of Poisson BF theory.

Appendix E
Some identities involving Pochhammer symbols

In this appendix, we review some identities involving Pochhammer symbols that are used in the calculation of holography chiral algebra. In the main text, we introduced the descending Pochhammer symbols

[a]_{n}:=a(a-1)\dots(a-n+1)=\frac{(a)!}{(a-n)!}.

(E.1)

We also introduce the ascending Pochhammer symbol

(a)^{(n)}:=a(a+1)\dots(a+n-1)=\frac{(a+n-1)!}{(a-1)!}.

(E.2)

The descending and ascending Pochhammer symbols are related to one another by

(a)^{(n)}=[a+n-1]_{n}.

(E.3)

The hypergeometric function
\prescript{~}{2}{F}_{1}
is defined as a power series using the ascending Pochhammer symbol

\prescript{~}{2}{F}_{1}(a,b,c,z)=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{(a)^{(i)}(b)^{(i)}}{(c)^{(i)}}\frac{1}{i!}z^{i}.

(E.4)

The series terminates if either
a
or
b
is a nonpositive integer, in which case the function reduces to a polynomial:

\prescript{~}{2}{F}_{1}(-k,b,c,z)=\sum_{i=0}^{k}(-1)^{i}\binom{k}{i}\frac{(b)^{(i)}}{(c)^{(i)}}z^{i}.

(E.5)

The following result is important in obtaining various generalizations of the Chu–Vandermonde’s identity.

Proposition E.1

(
[
68
]
)
.

For any
k\geq 1
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, and
a,b>0
, we have

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{k}{i}x^{i}y^{n-i}(a)^{(i)}(b)^{(n-i)}=y^{k}(a+b)^{(k)}\prescript{~}{2}{F}_{1}(-k,a,a+b,1-\frac{x}{y}).

(E.6)

Taking
x,y=1
in the above formula we obtain the Chu–Vandermonde’s identity

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{n}{i}(a)^{(i)}(b)^{(k-i)}=(a+b)^{(k)}.

(E.7)

Corollary E.1
.

\sum_{i=l}^{n}\binom{i}{l}\binom{k}{i}(a)^{(i)}(b)^{(k-i)}=\binom{k}{l}\frac{(a+b)^{(k)}}{(a+b)^{(l)}}(a)^{(l)}.

(E.8)

Proof.

Letting
x\to 1+x,y\to 1
in the formula
E.6
, we obtain the following

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{k}{i}(1+x)^{i}(a)^{(i)}(b)^{(k-i)}=(a+b)^{(k)}\prescript{~}{2}{F}_{1}(-n,a,a+b,-x).

(E.9)

Expanding both side into a series of
x
we obtain the formula
E.8
.
∎

Corollary E.2
.

We have the following identity

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\frac{1}{[a]_{i}[b]_{k-i}}=\frac{[a+b-k+1]_{k}}{[a]_{k}[b]_{k}}.

(E.10)

Proof.

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\frac{1}{[a]_{i}[b]_{k-i}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\frac{(a-k+k-i)!(b-k+i)!}{a!b!}

(E.11)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\frac{(a-k+1)^{(k-i)}(a-k)!(b-k+1)^{(i)}(b-k)!}{a!b!}

\displaystyle=(a+b-2k+2)^{(k)}\frac{(a-k)!(b-k)!}{a!b!}

\displaystyle=\frac{[a+b-k+1]_{k}}{[a]_{k}[b]_{k}},

where we used the Chu–Vandermonde’s identity
E.7
in the third line.
∎

Corollary E.3
.

We have the following identity

\sum_{i=l}^{k}\binom{i}{l}\binom{k}{i}\frac{1}{[a]_{i}[b]_{k-i}}=\binom{k}{l}\frac{[a+b-k+1]_{k}}{[a]_{k-l}[b]_{k}[a+b-2k+l+1]_{l}}.

(E.12)

Proof.

\displaystyle\sum_{i=l}^{k}\binom{i}{l}\binom{k}{i}\frac{1}{[a]_{i}[b]_{k-i}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=l}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\binom{i}{l}\frac{(a-k+1)^{(k-i)}(a-k)!(b-k+1)^{(i)}(b-k)!}{a!b!}

(E.13)

\displaystyle=\binom{k}{l}\frac{(a-k)!(b-k)!}{a!b!}\frac{(a+b-2k+2)^{(k)}}{(a+b-2k+2)^{(l)}}(a-k+1)^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\binom{k}{l}\frac{[a+b-k+1]_{k}}{[a]_{k-l}[b]_{k}[a+b-2k+l+1]_{l}}.

∎

Appendix F
tree-level Feynman integrals

In this appendix, we evaluate the tree-level Feynman diagrams that appear in Section
9
.

I_{k}(z)=\int_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C},s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}+s^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{z^{\prime}}^{k}\frac{1}{(|z^{\prime}|^{2}+s^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{z}sdsdz^{\prime}d\bar{z}^{\prime}.

(F.1)

First we note that
z^{k+1}I_{k}(z)
is a constant independent of
z
. To see this we shift
z\to\alpha z,\bar{z}\to\bar{\alpha}\bar{z}
. We also shift the integration variable by
z^{\prime}\to\alpha z^{\prime},\bar{z}^{\prime}\to\bar{\alpha}\bar{z}^{\prime},s\to|\alpha|s
.
z^{k+1}I_{k}(z)
is invariant under this shift. Therefore
z^{k+1}I_{k}(z)
is a constant and we have

I_{k}(z)=\frac{I_{k}(1)}{z^{k+1}}.

(F.2)

To efficiently evaluate all
I_{k}(1)
, we make the following generating function

I(1,\lambda):=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!}I_{k}(1).

(F.3)

By definition we have

I(1,\lambda)=\int_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C},s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(|1-z^{\prime}|^{2}+s^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{1}{((z^{\prime}+\lambda)\bar{z}^{\prime}+s^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}sdsdz^{\prime}d\bar{z}^{\prime}.

(F.4)

We use Schwinger parametrization to rewrite the above integral as follows

I(1,\lambda)=\frac{4}{\pi}\int_{u_{1},u_{2}\geq 0}du_{1}du_{2}\int_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C},s\geq 0}dsdz^{\prime}d\bar{z}^{\prime}(u_{1}u_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}se^{-u_{1}(|1-z^{\prime}|^{2}+s^{2})-u_{2}((z^{\prime}+\lambda)\bar{z}^{\prime}+s^{2})}.

(F.5)

We first evaluate the integral over
s
and find

I(1,\lambda)=\frac{4}{\pi}\int_{u_{1},u_{2}\geq 0}du_{1}du_{2}\int_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}}dz^{\prime}d\bar{z}^{\prime}\frac{1}{2(u_{1}+u_{2})}(u_{1}u_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-u_{1}|1-z^{\prime}|^{2}-u_{2}(z^{\prime}+\lambda)\bar{z}^{\prime}}.

(F.6)

Evaluate the integral over
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}
gives

I(1,\lambda)=2\int_{u_{1},u_{2}\geq 0}du_{1}du_{2}\frac{1}{(u_{1}+u_{2})^{2}}(u_{1}u_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{u_{1}u_{2}(1+\lambda)}{(u_{1}+u_{2})}}.

(F.7)

We make the following change of variable to the Schwinger parameter

u_{1}=u\xi,\;u_{2}=u(1-\xi),\;\text{ for }u\geq 0,1\leq\xi\leq 1.

(F.8)

Then we have

\displaystyle I(1,\lambda)

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\int_{0}^{\infty}du(\xi(1-\xi))^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-(1+\lambda)u\xi(1-\xi)}

(F.9)

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\frac{1}{(1+\lambda)\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{1+\lambda}.

We find that

I_{k}(z)=(-1)^{k}\frac{k!}{z^{k+1}}.

(F.10)

More generally, we can consider the following Feynman integral

I_{l,k}(z)=\int_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C},s\geq 0}\partial_{z^{\prime}}^{l}\frac{1}{(|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}+s^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{z^{\prime}}^{k}\frac{1}{(|z^{\prime}|^{2}+s^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{z}sdsdz^{\prime}d\bar{z}^{\prime}.

(F.11)

This can be evaluated using integration by part to move the
\partial_{z^{\prime}}^{l}
derivatives to the second position and using the previous result. We find

I_{l,k}(z)=(-1)^{k}\frac{(k+l)!}{z^{k+l+1}}.

(F.12)

Perimeter Institute for Theoretical Physics, Waterloo, Canada
